- A ladder of offers at different price points captures buyers across the funnel. Free content comes first, a low-ticket paid event or product second, and a high-ticket offer last. Most people take the free step, a smaller share buys the middle offer, and only a rare few buy the top offer. Each step gets a sale from a different share of the audience. [L0919.040]
- A deeper funnel with more offers increases what each customer is worth, which raises what you can afford to spend to acquire one. The business that can outspend competitors on customer acquisition usually wins the market. Plan funnel depth with acquisition costs in mind. [L0523.018]
- A sales funnel is the online sequence of pages and messages that moves a prospect up the value ladder. Bait sits at the top, and each step narrows the audience to fewer, higher-value buyers. The funnel turns the ladder into a working sales process. [L0523.017]
- Case: A fitness media company had 1.2 million Facebook followers but lost 90% of its traffic after platform changes. It was spending 116 USD to sell a product priced at 97 USD and was no longer profitable. The real problem was not traffic or conversion but the absence of a value ladder and a sales funnel, so customers had no clear next step after their first purchase. Once the company added offers that let customers move up, it could afford to pay two to three times more per sale, outbid competitors for traffic and gain more customers and sales. [L0523.016]
- Study how successful brands in your niche structure tiered offers, bundle names and urgency tactics. Test variations of what converts for competitors in your market. [L0232.019]
- A free ebook bundled with a multi-unit offer serves two goals: it adds perceived value and moves the buyer from one unit to at least two. It works best when the ebook delivers real value on its own, so the gift does not feel like a sales pitch. [L0862.032]
- An offer like buy one get one free can drive strong click-through rates and sales. The store must have a reliable supplier that ships the exact product sold, otherwise the store can collapse under refunds. [L0780.036]
- The instant giveaway funnel is expected to stay effective for roughly six to twelve months before it saturates and stops working. Plan a replacement strategy before that point. [L0797.022]

### Funnel stages and when to build a funnel

- Funnel stages run awareness, consideration, conversion, loyalty and advocacy. The TOFU, MOFU and BOFU labels mark the top, middle and bottom of the funnel. Each stage loses people, so only a small share of those who see an ad ends up buying. This is why the funnel narrows. [L0919.002]
- Build a new funnel from scratch only when no working funnel exists. Improving a funnel that already makes sales takes far less effort than building one. [L0378.005]
- Higher-ticket dropshipping products (around $200 to $300 and above) need more trust, consultative selling and a longer customer journey. Paid ads alone are expensive for such products, so a warm-up content path is often needed first. [L0552.036]
- Warm-up funnel for a higher-ticket product: [L0552.037]
  1. Create an advertorial or educational landing page that addresses the customer's problem.
  2. Or publish a YouTube video explaining the problem and solution.
  3. Retarget viewers who watched a set share of the video, for example 75%, with an offer.
  4. For more expensive items, first sell a lower-ticket item, then move the customer into an email list and make the larger offer later.

### Advertorials and editorials

- Sending cold paid traffic straight to a product page asks visitors to trust a brand they have never seen. Only visitors who already know their problem and want a solution tend to buy. An editorial step lets the store convert the rest of the paid audience. [L0578.002]
- Audience segments for a product, approximately:

  | Segment | Share of audience | Role of editorial | Source |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Impulse buyers who know their problem and look for a solution | About 10-15% or less | Go straight to the product page; product pages mainly convert this group | [L0578.003] |
  | Potential prospects who partly understand their problem, may have objections and trust little | 35-70% | Main group an editorial should convert | [L0578.004] |
  | Not interested in the product | About 10-20% | Editorial content is not worth aiming at this group | [L0578.005] |

- Plan editorial content around the three audience groups. Impulse buyers go straight to the product page, potential prospects need the editorial, and the uninterested group is not worth targeting. This keeps effort focused on the largest group that product pages miss. [L0578.032]
- Giving value before selling builds authority. Trusting customers tend to accept higher prices and upsell or cross-sell offers more easily, which can raise average order value. [L0578.008]
- Host editorials on your own website rather than on third-party publishers. Own-site editorials convert well and keep the traffic in your store. Third-party placements mainly add authority, not conversions. [L0578.028]
- Third-party publisher placements in magazines or newsletters in your niche can add authority and bring better-quality traffic, and the article can be reused on your store. They usually require a publication fee, and the publisher must agree that the product and claims are good and accurate. [L0578.029]
- Sending cold ad traffic first to an advertorial that educates about the problem, and only then to the offer page, is presented as a way to raise conversion. Visitors arrive more aware of the problem and more convinced of the solution, so they are warmer buyers. [L1131.001]
- Advertorial funnel flow: [L1131.002]
  1. Ad with a relatable story about a problem the buyer recognizes.
  2. Click leads to an advertorial that educates about the problem and general solutions.
  3. A call-to-action button leads to the offer page.
  4. Checkout.
  5. After purchase, an upsell page offers extra products at a discount.
  6. Visitors who started checkout but did not buy receive an abandoned-cart email.
  7. Advertorial visitors who did not click through to the offer page are retargeted with an ad that links directly to the offer page, not back to the advertorial.
- Advertorials fit products where the buyer can be educated about a benefit or a problem before buying, such as comfortable shoes linked to foot or body health. They fit poorly for products with no educational angle. [L1131.003]
- An advertorial funnel only makes sense for products around 40 to 50 dollars or more, where the buyer thinks harder about spending and the return on ad spend and margin can carry the extra steps. A 5-dollar ebook does not justify it. [L1131.004]

### Creative strategy

- Modern dropshipping shifted from making every ad yourself to adapting proven creatives. Start from ads that have already run for weeks, verify them in the ad library and then adapt them to your product. [L1479.025]
- Use the Facebook Ad Library to find winning creatives. If the same creative appears in 10 to 15 ads, the advertiser is scaling it. Adapt that creative to your market, for example with a French voice-over. [L1436.078]
- Build the creative strategy in this order: [L0902.005]
  1. Research the target audience and customer psychology in depth.
  2. Research competitors and affinity brands in depth.
  3. Map best-selling products, current winning products and offers.

  Focus this research on finding marketing angles and ad angles.
- The choice is between speed (rip existing content and test it fast) and customization (build your own content and ads). For a brand meant to scale, full customization is the recommended route, because ripped content gives no lasting edge and carries legal risk. [L0794.033]
- Brands can set their own red lines, such as never attacking competitors, not using certain pain points, or not creating fictional UGC creators. Fewer self-imposed restrictions usually give a stronger creative strategy, because more variations can be tested and ad platforms look for that variety. [L0464.021]
- Limits from the niche, funding, existing team and required approvals determine which parts of a creative system a brand should build. A brand may need only a few sections rather than the whole system. [L0464.022]
- The purpose of creative strategy is to make ads. Any process, tool or system that does not contribute to producing ads has no value. [L0464.023]
- Split creative effort by output type. Workhorses are the main production focus, and warhorses are an occasional addition. Relevance and volume of low-effort ads drive most results. [L0483.007]
- Problem-focused video ads that present the problem and then the solution can send viewers to a store. The creator intended to test this with AI-made ads. The results of such ads are only known after testing, so they should be measured rather than assumed. [L0888.004]
- A new dropshipping brand has no brand equity, so ads must sell to strangers. Strong products can sometimes sell with weak marketing, but in competitive markets a seller needs stronger persuasion and craft to connect with buyers. [L1348.003]
- Copywriting in e-commerce is persuasive selling in written or spoken form that replaces the salesperson who is absent from online stores. Its job is to walk the visitor through deciding whether the product fits their problem or desire. It applies to product pages, sales pages, ads, emails and video scripts alike. [L1460.001] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Case: Security Rare Coin achieved a 3,400% increase in sales (from $3.5 million to over $120 million in a little over a year) through copywriting centered on strong bullets and fascinations based on control marketing techniques. This demonstrates the scalability potential of systematic bullet writing applied to product promotion. [L0068.042]

### AI-supported creative systems

- Method for building a better offer and improving product naming with an AI assistant: [L0683.019]
  1. Ask the AI to apply the Magic Headline Formula to your product and produce several product name options.
  2. Ask it to write a product description using the same formula.
  3. Edit both outputs yourself to add a human touch, rather than copying them directly.
  4. Test the candidate names against each other to see which performs best.
- Prompt for a lean AI setup for ad creation. Use it as written: [L0613.064]

```
Create a lean AI setup that helps our brand make ads that are more effective and data-backed, and expand faster. Main functions: 1. Audience understanding: review surveys, personas and behavioral data; pinpoint genuine pain points, desires, objections and purchase drivers; detect new trends across audience segments. 2. Market and rival analysis: investigate competitors and where the industry is heading; find gaps and openings so the brand can stand apart and position itself more firmly. 3. Creative direction and brainstorming: produce fresh ad angles, hooks and creative ideas from customer findings; sharpen existing concepts by anchoring them in data and established marketing frameworks. 4. Scripts and copy: draft persuasive, high-converting ad scripts and marketing text; check and polish copy so it fits the audience's emotions, vocabulary and motives. 5. Ad results feedback cycle: assess performance (ROAS, CTR, hook rate, watch time, CPC and similar); explain why some ads succeed and others underperform; convert those lessons into practical guidance for the next batch of creatives. 6. Knowledge base linkage: treat all uploaded files and research as the core knowledge base; automatically apply insights and 'Breakthrough Advertising' principles to all output; keep answers tied to actual audience data. Value for the founder: turns customer feedback into clear, usable insights; proposes creative angles and strategies suited to the audience; speeds up writing better ads using real data; drives ongoing improvement by analyzing ad outcomes and closing the creative loop.
```

## Growth levers: AOV, repeat purchases and customer lifetime value

### Three growth levers

- A business grows in three ways: winning more first-time buyers, raising the value of each order, and getting existing customers to buy again and more often. Check each lever separately. [L0378.003]
- (older, 2021-22) Store growth has three levers: more first-time customers through traffic or conversion rate, higher average order value or profit per order through higher-priced products, bundles or better margins, and a higher repeat purchase rate. Pulling one lever alone yields linear growth, while pulling all three at once yields exponential growth. [L0414.003]
- (older, 2021-22) Any e-commerce business can grow in only three ways: attract more first-time customers or traffic, raise the average order value, or raise the rate of repeat purchases. Each customer who buys again adds to growth without a new acquisition. [L0482.001]
- (older, 2021-22) The third lever is selling more than once to the same customer. Repeat sales from existing customers are a separate growth path from acquiring new ones. [L0448.002]
- Focus on lifetime value (LTV) before average order value (AOV). First identify the channel or action that makes the most money and push it until its return stops growing before moving resources to another channel. [L0334.023]

### Lifetime value and repeat customers

- Customer lifetime value (CLV) and lifetime value (LTV) determine long-term success. Reacquiring existing customers costs far less than acquiring new ones, so focus on repeat customers, not just first-order volume. [L0324.003]
- Do not treat dropshipping as a short-term cash grab where customer experience does not matter. Running out of new customers without retention systems leads to business collapse. [L0324.004]
- Operating a successful business centers on creating exceptional customer experiences that build satisfaction. Satisfied customers become repeat buyers and advocates. [L0028.008]
- (older, 2021-22) Selling again to an existing customer is usually easier and more profitable than acquiring a new one. If repeat sales are made without the same marketing cost as a new customer, the profit margin on the second or third sale is often higher. [L0482.002]
- (older, 2021-22) Repeat customers cost nothing extra to acquire again, you can email them directly, and they already trust your business. This makes repeat sales cheaper and more profitable than finding new buyers each time. [L0994.019]
- (older, 2021-22) Recurring revenue means the same customer buys from you more than once. Repeat business is a core way to grow because repeat sales are easier to close, cost less to market, and usually carry higher profit margins on the second and third order onward. [L1485.001, L1486.001]
- (older, 2021-22) A store that sells only a single one-time product must keep finding new buyers and cannot market profitably to existing customers. Its growth is capped far below that of a store that can sell to the same customers again and again. [L1486.002]
- (older, 2021-22) Large e-commerce companies such as Amazon grow mainly because customers return to buy other products. Without repeat purchases they would exhaust their market and stagnate, and the same risk applies to a small store. [L1486.003]
- Returning customers usually contribute about 20% to 30% of revenue in dropshipping, and up to 40% in good cases. Subscription boxes and consumable products can reach much higher repeat rates. [L1213.078]
- In most niches more than 80 percent of store visitors are first-time visitors, so repeat purchase rates stay low. Plan customer acquisition for new buyers, and use upsells, cross-sells, referral discounts for family and friends, and affiliate content for indirectly related products to add value per customer. [L1283.108]
- The 20% to 30% figure measures share of revenue, while the 80% figure measures share of visitors. Both can hold at the same time in one store. [L1213.078, L1283.108]

### Customer multiplier funnel

- A customer multiplier funnel is a series of actions aimed at existing customers with two goals: selling more products after the first purchase, and turning customers into people who bring in other customers. It is not a paid advertising method such as pay-per-click. [L0482.003]
- (older, 2021-22) Upselling is the first stage of a customer multiplier funnel. Onsells and resells follow the same logic: they aim at a second purchase from customers who have already bought once, so revenue per existing customer rises. [L0448.023]
- (older, 2021-22) Upsells and resells happen only when they are deliberately planned and systematically executed. Repeat purchases cannot be left to chance or to customer loyalty alone. [L0448.011]
- (older, 2021-22) Some funnel actions take a long time to pay off, while others can be set up once and then keep producing results. Immediately actionable steps can be put in place within days or weeks. [L0482.005]
- (older, 2021-22) For most businesses that already make sales, have no other major problems, and can implement these actions, a customer multiplier funnel is expected to add 10 to 20 percent in additional revenue per month. [L0482.006]
- (older, 2021-22) Formula:
  Expected additional monthly revenue from a customer multiplier funnel = current monthly revenue × 10% to 20%
  Example: at 100,000 USD monthly revenue, the funnel is expected to add 10,000 to 20,000 USD per month. [L0482.007]
- Once a customer has made a purchase, run three follow-up sequences at the same time: a brand ambassador program, lead value optimization offers for related products or services, and email campaigns that bring buyers back to the store. Set these up after the first sale rather than treating any one of them as a separate tactic. [L0676.017]

### Upsells, cross-sells and expanding the offer

- Expanding the offer means adding more products to a store and building better offers such as bundles, upsells and cross-sells. The goal is a higher average order value and more bottom-line profit. [L0434.001, L0435.001]
- Complementary products sold alongside a main product, either at cart level or as a bundle, increase the chance that the customer buys the second item. The stronger the relation between the products, such as pillowcases and hair accessories for the same audience, the higher this chance. [L0434.006]
- After finding a winning product, sell different variants and cross-sells of it to raise average order value. Each additional item in a single order improves profit per customer. [L0562.026]
- Upsells and cross-sells raise average order value and margin from a customer who is already buying, so they suit any store, including one with a single product. Add them at some level even when the store only sells one item. [L1412.001]
- (older, 2021-22) Accessories with a lower price can be added to an expensive main product to raise the average order value and reach a broader customer base at a higher margin. [L1481.007]
- (older, 2021-22) Pellet grill accessories can serve as upsells and downsells, and utensils or cedar planks can serve as lead magnets. A lead magnet offer sent to a content page can explain the benefits of owning a pellet grill to someone who has a normal grill. [L1152.017]
- Selling one product only is fine, but selling several products can create trust and enable bundles. Monitor which products competitor stores sell most and test bundles based on those winners. [L0773.046]
- A niche store can run ads on one winning product while other products are promoted to existing customers through email. Adding products to the same niche can be a second step after the current winner has been scaled. [L0694.032]

### Money machines

- A money machine is a repeatable process that converts prospects into customers or deepens customer relationships. It is designed to run on autopilot and multiply revenue through lead capture, upselling, cross-selling, retention and remarketing. [L0240.001]
- A money machine is an automated process that takes people in and pays money out, bolted onto the core store, which stays the main sales engine. Five types cover the customer journey:

| Type | Stage of the journey |
|---|---|
| Lead generation | Before the store |
| Cross-sell | Product pages |
| Upsell | Checkout |
| Post-purchase | After buying |
| Remarketing | After leaving the site, whether or not the visitor bought |

  [L1076.001]
- A money machine should lift average order value by about 5 to 10 percent. A store with a 1,000 dollar AOV should be able to reach roughly 1,050 to 1,100 dollars. [L1076.002]
- Money machines are built to raise average order value, customer lifetime value and net profit per customer. Most run on autopilot once set up. Each one needs a stated setup time and budget before you decide when to build it. [L1076.003]
- Setting up a lead generation machine:
  1. Write the lead magnet content in a Google Doc.
  2. Outsource the design and add footers and links.
  3. Build the landing page with the free form or a builder.
  4. Connect the sign-up to Klaviyo and build the flow.
  5. Send traffic from blog banners, sponsorships, Facebook ads or influencers.
  6. Email at least twice a week and check the Klaviyo revenue after one to two months.

  [L1076.023]

### Lead value optimization (LVO)

- Lead value optimization (LVO) means increasing what each customer is worth over time through cross-selling related products, affiliate offers and promotions throughout the year. It is a separate lever from referral traffic and can be combined with it. [L0417.003]
- Lead value optimization means earning more from every visitor, whether or not they buy the core product. Conversion rate optimization focuses on getting visitors to buy the core product. Start lead value optimization only after the store has real sales, because that is when the extra offers start to pay off. [L0923.001]
- High-spending competitors on top paid search placements can stay profitable because they earn more from each lead than the core product sale alone. If a lead costs about 3 dollars per click, the store needs back-end or side-funnel revenue from that lead to cover the cost. [L0923.002]
- Cross-promoting your own niche stores is the easiest way to raise the value of each lead. Buyers who just purchased are the most receptive to another offer. Most stores with strong sales still skip this step, even though it reuses an existing audience at almost no cost. [L0923.012]

### Consumables and subscriptions

- High-margin consumables are the most valuable product type for repeat revenue. If a consumable such as a filter or accessory can be added to the business, add it quickly by talking to the supplier. If no consumable fits, sell a warranty instead. [L0334.025]
- (older, 2021-22) Consumable products such as protein powder give buyers a reason to reorder. A customer won through a low-cost entry product can become worth much more over time than a one-time buyer, which is why a break-even front-end sale can still be profitable. [L0511.025]
- At a low unit price, a single-item order rarely pays for paid acquisition, so a consumable product needs bundles or a subscription to raise average order value. Repeat purchases of a consumable make the customer worth far more than the first order. [L0524.001]
- A typical adult uses roughly 17 to 20 bars of soap per year, and some sources cite up to 26. For conservative planning, use 17 units per year. [L0524.002]
- (older, 2021-22) Consumable products that customers reorder, such as filament, can be sold on a subscription basis to create recurring revenue. [L1152.013]
- Monthly recurring revenue (MRR) comes from subscription systems and recurring models. It is the step that takes a store beyond one-time sales. [L0914.013]
- A subscription app such as Recharge or the Shopify subscription payments app on a competitor store signals a subscription-based model. Check how the competitor sells its subscription when you model recurring revenue. [L1164.019]
- Subscription upgrades in the cart raise customer lifetime value by turning one-time buyers into subscribers. Buy More Save More raises average order value by encouraging customers to put more quantity into each order. [L1231.004]
- Peak sales periods such as Black Friday and Cyber Monday bring much more traffic, so cart features that convert visitors quickly become more important during those weeks. Letting shoppers upgrade a one-time item to a subscription even after it is in the cart is a key example. [L1231.005]

### Loyalty

- (older, 2021-22) High-ticket stores can still get repeat purchases if the catalog includes several products a customer might buy at different times. A loyalty program adds a reason to come back by giving customers a reward they can spend on their next order. [L0956.001]
- When a customer does a favor for the brand, such as posting a photo or writing a review, they become more connected to the brand, remember the experience more and are more likely to buy again and recommend the store. The request itself therefore builds loyalty, not only content. [L0417.007]

### Referral programs

- (older, 2021-22) Referrals from existing customers compound over time. If each customer brings in another customer, sales and revenue roughly double in theory, though in practice only some customers will refer others. The effect grows as more new customers send more customers. [L0406.001]
- (older, 2021-22) Recommendations from a customer's friends or family are trusted more than a business's own advertising. Customers who refer others bring in new business at a much lower direct cost than paid marketing. [L0406.002]
- A post-purchase referral system can be started from the very first sale, because it only needs a customer who has already bought. It turns a normal one-way sales funnel into an hourglass by sending buyers back out as a new traffic source. [L0417.001]
- The referral approach is not an affiliate or paid-referrer program and not a multi-level structure. Buyers are not paid to recruit. The goal is to give every customer a strong experience and then ask for specific actions that naturally lead to word of mouth. [L0417.004]
- Referral traffic is not expected to be the largest traffic source and usually will not make up most of the revenue. Word of mouth is still very effective, and the big advantage is that the system only has to be set up once and then runs on its own. [L0417.005]
- Choose the post-purchase ask based on product type and buyer profile:

| Product type | Suitable ask |
|---|---|
| Products people like to show off (trendy, cool, hobby or design items) | Photo and social post requests |
| Products people do not want to show publicly (medical supplies, wheelchairs, products for an older demographic) | Review requests |
| Mixed range | Many stores use both |

  [L0417.006]
- (older, 2021-22) Customers who feel well looked after are more likely to recommend a store to others. A referral program adds a deliberate nudge on top of good service. Rewarding each successful referral costs little extra and makes the recommendation more likely. [L1234.001]
- (older, 2021-22) Set up a referral program for the store and reward every successful referral. Customers must know about the reward before they refer anyone. [L1234.002]
- (older, 2021-22) A referral program can run as a separate system or be integrated with the loyalty program. How the two work together depends on the tool chosen. [L1234.003]
- (older, 2021-22) Referral rewards can be loyalty points redeemable for future purchases, a discount on the store, or cash. Each option suits a different business model and should be chosen deliberately. [L1234.010]
- (older, 2021-22) There is no single right way to reward referrals. One example is a cash affiliate payout made once every 30 days, covering referrals from the previous 30 days. [L1234.012]
- (older, 2021-22) Choose the reward structure while configuring the app, based on what fits the business best. Expect to test and adjust it over time, as with any marketing activity. [L1234.013]
- (older, 2021-22) Create a dedicated referral page on the website that explains how to sign up and how to refer others. Link this page in the site footer so customers can always find it. [L1234.016]
- (older, 2021-22) Referral program procedure:
  1. Implement a referral program for the store.
  2. Pick a referral tool, exploring the available options and choosing the one that fits best.
  3. Set up the chosen tool in the business.
  4. Build the referral emails into the email marketing system.

  [L1234.018]

### Promotions calendar logic

- The growth plan has three parts that run together over 12 months: build audiences, create offers that are hard to refuse, and promote those offers to the audiences. The aim is more sales and profit that you can predict. [L0757.001]
- Plan monthly promotions so they add roughly 20 to 30 percent of monthly revenue on top of regular sales. This gives a sales floor that still works if paid ads are turned off or organic traffic drops. [L1204.001]
- The system is meant to add 20 to 30 percent to monthly revenue, according to the lesson. Treat that figure as a target to measure against, not a guaranteed result. [L0757.004]
- Dropping monthly promotions after one underperforming offer is a mistake. Consistent testing shows which offers a given audience buys, and the contact pool grows with each month. [L1204.002]
- Shoppers who did not buy during dynamic remarketing (about 7 to 30 days) or after an abandoned-cart email flow (up to about 9 days in Klaviyo) still form a warm pool. Monthly offers are a way to reach that pool again. [L1204.003]
- Promotions aimed at existing audiences should not be started on day one. They become worthwhile once traffic, remarketing audiences and the email list have grown. Core traffic sources such as Google Ads, Facebook Ads, Microsoft Ads and SEO should come first. [L1205.001]
- Peak periods such as Black Friday and Cyber Monday are the weeks when cart-level conversion features matter most. [L1231.005]
- Next steps for the growth plan:
  1. Write down at least 4 offers you can promote to your audience.
  2. Assign them on a rotating basis across the next 12 months.
  3. Create offer assets: emails and ads for each offer.
  4. Schedule the offers in Klaviyo, Google and Facebook.
  5. Modify future offers based on the results of each monthly promotion.
  6. Track whether the system adds 20 to 30 percent to monthly revenue.

  [L0757.042]

### Margin improvement priorities

- Profit margin is the most common financial problem for dropshipping stores once they reach a certain size. Growing revenue without fixing margin multiplies the problems along with the sales. [L1046.040]
- (older, 2021-22) Growing revenue at a 9 percent net margin brings little extra profit for a lot of extra effort, so raising gross margin is usually the better lever. In the example, a flat-revenue year with 20 percent gross margin would be worth more than doubling sales at the current margin. [L1046.041]
- (older, 2021-22) More orders means more work and more operational risk, while margin gains bring cash without extra workload. Choose improvements that raise profit per order before spending on sales growth. [L1046.042]
- (older, 2021-22) Margin improvement priorities, in order:
  1. Find new suppliers and product categories with higher margins, and add only products that clear the minimum margin including shipping.
  2. Renegotiate pricing with the current top suppliers, using the volume they receive from the store as leverage.
  3. Renegotiate shipping costs with suppliers or freight providers.
  4. Test price increases on products where a small volume loss is acceptable.
  5. Limit or stop discounts, including custom quotes.
  6. Move marketing and SEO to maintenance mode and keep the current sales level while putting energy into margin.

  [L1046.043]
- (older, 2021-22) Stores that scale often run out of capacity to take new orders and stop adding suppliers and products. The fix is to keep adding products, but to choose suppliers selectively by margin. [L1046.044]

### Discounts and offers

- (older, 2021-22) A discount shrinks margin, but losing a customer you could have won usually costs more. A deal can be the right call when it closes a sale that would otherwise go to a competitor. Every sale you make also limits your competitors' budgets, because they cannot outspend you on marketing without revenue. [L0799.001]

### Sequencing and guardrails

- Do not start customizations such as post-purchase surveys, feedback loops or one-click upsells until the concept is approved and the business is profitable. Share your P&L before moving to these steps. Concept approval and profitability come first, then customization. [L0906.020]

### Reference figures

Where sources give different values, each applies to a different scope.

| Figure | Value | Applies to | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Customer multiplier funnel uplift (older, 2021-22) | 10% to 20% of current monthly revenue | Businesses that already make sales, have no other major problems, and can implement the actions | L0482.006, L0482.007 |
| Money machine AOV uplift | About 5% to 10% | Each money machine on the core store | L1076.002 |
| Monthly promotions share | About 20% to 30% of monthly revenue, on top of regular sales | Promotions calendar as a sales floor | L1204.001, L0757.004 |
| Returning customers share of revenue | About 20% to 30%, up to 40% in good cases | Dropshipping stores | L1213.078 |
| First-time visitors share | More than 80% of visitors | Most niches | L1283.108 |
| Soap units per year | 17 to 20 typical, up to 26 cited; plan with 17 | Consumable planning example | L0524.002 |
| Net margin example (older, 2021-22) | 9% net margin, compared with 20% gross margin in the example | Margin versus sales growth comparison | L1046.041 |

## Relationship-based growth: Dream 100, partners, affiliates and influencers

### Why relationship-based growth works

- In any established market, established players already control customer attention. Build relationships with these authorities and leverage their credibility rather than building all your trust independently from scratch. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0297.001]
- Customer attention directly drives revenue. Whoever has the most attention in a market can command the highest prices and attract the most sales. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0297.002]
- Relationship-based growth is a distinct channel from direct marketing methods such as Google Ads, social media ads and email marketing, but it is equally powerful for customer acquisition. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0297.003]
- Most e-commerce businesses, especially high-ticket dropshipping businesses, operate in market isolation and miss the partnership and collaboration opportunities available within their industry. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0297.005]
- Established figures in your market are willing to collaborate in mutually beneficial partnerships and share audience access, because both parties gain value from the relationship. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0297.006]
- Relationship-based business growth is a traditional, centuries-proven method that has fueled successful enterprises. It remains underutilized in modern e-commerce despite its effectiveness. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0297.008]
- High-ticket dropshippers usually enter established markets rather than creating them. The brands or products may have existed online or offline for 10 to 40 years or more, so influential voices and audiences that solve the same problems already exist to partner with. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0412.005]

### Transferred influence and intent-based traffic

- Transferred influence means borrowing the credibility and existing audience of people or entities that already have influence in your niche, so they send traffic to your store. It adds a traffic source on top of search traffic, where shoppers look for your products, brand or SKU on Google or Bing. [L0528.001]
- Intent-based traffic comes from shoppers who already search for a product or brand. Partner-based traffic reaches people through trusted third parties where they already spend time. Both sources complement each other. [L0528.002]
- The top-ranked partner in a niche is usually a long-term target rather than a first contact. Outreach starts with the many smaller partners, because the goal is many relationships, not one big deal. [L0528.003]

### Dream 100 outreach and engagement

#### Principles

- Before you ask a high-value prospect for a partnership or sale, build familiarity first by engaging with their content over time. Direct cold outreach with a sales pitch tends to trigger a spam reaction and put people off. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0453.001]
- Engagement works because an audience-facing person notices and remembers those who interact with them repeatedly. When you later reach out directly, the contact feels natural rather than like a stranger's cold message. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0453.002]
- Engagement in this phase should give value and ask questions, not request anything. Do not ask for a sale or partnership yet. Help the prospect's audience where you can and show real interest in their work. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0453.014]
- Treat relationship building like a normal conversation with a new acquaintance. Learn what they do and what they are interested in, and engage with that. Stay genuine and do not pretend to be someone you are not. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0453.015]
- Dream 100 partnerships must give both sides a benefit, because a relationship that only serves one party tends to end. Frame every outreach as a win-win offer rather than a request for help. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.001]
- Use the Dream 100 exercise for high-value targets. Use the affiliate recruitment email template for general affiliate recruitment searches outside your Dream 100 list. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0212.008]
- Use the same outreach message whether you contact a potential affiliate by email or on social media (Facebook, Instagram and similar). The message content stays consistent across all contact channels. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0212.010]

#### Step-by-step engagement

Procedure (older advice, 2021-22):

1. Find your Dream 100 prospects and follow, subscribe to, or join the email list of each one.
2. Engage with their content on the channels where they are active: Facebook groups and forums, YouTube, Instagram, blogs and email.
3. Keep this up over time. Only after familiarity has built should you contact them directly about working together.

[L0453.003]

#### Channel-by-channel tactics

**Facebook groups and forums** (older advice, 2021-22)

- Engage by answering members' questions with useful, experience-based information from your market. Do not promote your product in replies, because promotional answers usually get you removed from the group. [L0453.005]
- Group and forum owners want questions answered without having to do it themselves. Consistent helpful replies are visible to admins, who can track the most engaged members, so this builds goodwill with the people who run the community. [L0453.006]
- At this stage, do not contact the owner or admin. Your target is regular, helpful participation in the community itself. [L0453.007]
- Facebook setup: keep the personal profile and the business page separate. The personal profile follows Dream 100 contacts, joins their groups and forums, and handles relationship-building. The business Facebook page is used only to interact with other pages you run. Groups that refuse page accounts will block a page that tries to join, so the page should never be the one that joins. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0526.012]

**YouTube** (older advice, 2021-22)

- When a prospect you follow publishes a new video, watch it and leave a comment. Either say what you liked about it, or ask a genuine question if the video taught you something and left you with one. [L0453.008]

**Instagram** (older advice, 2021-22)

- Check your feed for posts from your Dream 100 prospects from the last couple of days and comment on them. Say what you like, ask a question, or offer a relevant suggestion. You do not need to react to every single post. [L0453.009]

**YouTube and Instagram algorithms** (older advice, 2021-22)

- These algorithms give more reach to content that gets more engagement. Creators therefore value regular, genuine comments from people who engage with them, which is why consistent commenting builds goodwill. [L0453.010]

**Blogs and websites** (older advice, 2021-22)

- When a prospect publishes a new post, read it and leave a comment. Say what you found interesting, add your own view, or ask a question that invites the author to reply and continue the conversation. [L0453.011]

**Email** (older advice, 2021-22)

- Reply to the newsletters and emails your prospects send you. Thank them, say what you liked, or ask a question. Always sign with your name so they begin to recognise it. [L0453.012]
- Email is usually the best long-term channel for ongoing contact with a prospect, since prospects send newsletters to subscribers and replies keep the relationship active. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0453.013]

**Respectful disagreement** (older advice, 2021-22)

- When a prospect publishes something you disagree with, you may give respectful, constructive feedback in a comment. Thank them for the content and say what was good. Then explain clearly why you think a specific point is wrong, since most audience-building creators appreciate this. [L0453.018]

#### Working a prospect toward partnership

- Most Dream 100 prospects already earn from their audience through coaching, advertising on their site or forum, or their own products that do not compete with yours. If a prospect does not monetize, helping them grow their audience is still a valid offer. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.002]
- The list of monetization options is not exhaustive, and there are no fixed boundaries on what a partnership can be. The real task is a conversation about how both businesses can work together for mutual benefit. Any outcome both sides accept is the right one. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.003]
- A single prospect can be worked through several partnership types over time, not only one. Start with one option and add others as the relationship grows. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.004]

### Joint ventures and joint promotions

- Joint content production and promotion: create a piece of content together and promote it to both audiences, or have both sides run ads to the same content. Agree in advance on how much ad spend each side will contribute. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.007]
- A joint venture (JV) works with a prospect who sells a complementary product in the same market but is not a direct competitor. Build a deal where customers get a bundle of both products. The bundle does not have to be physically packed together. Promote it to both audiences. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.008]
- Joint ventures cross-pollinate audiences: each side's customers join the other side's list. This is why joint product promotions are attractive to both partners. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.009]
- A small ad purchase can be a starting point. For example, rent ad space for one month. If it works, the relationship can grow into ongoing deals such as sponsored emails or other joint promotions. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0525.031]

### Affiliate programs: why they work

- Affiliate programs let you leverage other people's audiences to drive sales. You pay commissions only when a sale completes. You never pay for traffic itself, which makes affiliate marketing less risky than paid advertising, where you pay for clicks regardless of outcome. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0213.001]
- Affiliates generate qualified traffic by recommending your products to an existing audience that already trusts them on a particular topic. Their audience's trust transfers to your brand when they make a recommendation. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0213.003]
- Affiliates naturally recommend only products that align with their audience, because content creation requires substantial effort and investment. Poor-fit recommendations waste their work, generate no sales or commission, and risk burning the audience trust that is their primary business asset. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0213.019]
- Affiliate marketing works best as an extra revenue stream added to a dropshipping store, not as a standalone business. [L0359.004]
- Affiliate marketing is an optional add-on for a store that is already live and profitable. It should not be a requirement before launch or before the first sales. (older advice, 2019) [L1068.010]
- An older affiliate website with no traffic and no audience is usually better left aside, and a new store should be started with this model. An active site with real traffic, for example 1,000 visitors a day, can be extended with the model. (older advice, 2019) [L1068.011]
- Affiliate programs suit stores that already do roughly $1 million or more in annual revenue, as a way to grow from about $1 million to $2 million. They are not a launch tactic for a new store. Refersion is the platform recommended for them. (older advice, 2022) [L1281.027]

### Affiliate programs: running your own

#### Commission rates by source

Sources disagree on the rate, because each one describes a different program or context. Use the row that matches your situation.

| Source | Rate or amount | When it applies | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| E-Converts, Source D 2026 | 25% of the complete membership amount, paid to members who successfully refer new members | Member referral program for a membership product | [L0214.001] |
| E-Converts payout timing, Source D 2026 | Paid within 48 hours of the referred member completing their membership purchase | Same program | [L0214.003] |
| Source A, refer program (older advice, 2021) | $250 per new customer enrolled through the refer program | The referral program described in that course | [L0270.001] |
| Source A, example (older advice, 2019) | $500 per sign-up on a $2,000 yoga retreat | Illustration of an affiliate offer on a site that sells yoga mats | [L1002.005] |
| Source A (older advice, 2021) | About 10 to 20% of sales generated through your affiliate link | Standard deals for content-led affiliate sites | [L1279.099] |
| Source A, 2023 | About 2 to 10% for physical products. Up to around 20% only rarely | Typical physical-product affiliate deals | [L0855.073] |

- Amazon Associates commissions were reduced to approximately 1 to 2% on most product categories. This creates a competitive opportunity for dropshippers to recruit Amazon affiliates by offering equal or superior commission rates. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0215.003]

#### Economics and incentive design

- The 25% commission rate is positioned as an investment in the business, not a cost. Paying high commissions for member referrals aligns the company's goal, helping people build real businesses, with member incentives to grow the community. [L0214.009]
- Case: A member referred a friend to E-Converts and received a $1,000 referral commission. He invested the entire amount as startup capital for a dropshipping store and is now generating approximately $100,000 in monthly revenue. [L0214.006]

#### Scope rules

- With affiliate sales, the customer buys from the brand, so you do not own the customer and cannot market to them later. [L0855.074]
- Affiliate promotions can run on a drop-ship store only for complementary products that do not compete with your own supplier products. Affiliate items earn a much smaller margin and cannot be advertised with the same Google Shopping campaigns as your drop-ship products. (older advice, 2021) [L0834.111]
- Google Shopping ads cannot promote products you do not sell directly, so affiliate products cannot be used in Shopping campaigns. [L0855.075]
- Affiliate offers are only worth adding for complementary products you do not sell, such as online lessons for products sold in the store. They should never be a competing product from the same niche. [L0855.077]
- Shopify stores can carry affiliate deals in two ways: promoting affiliate offers on the store, and partnering with affiliates who send traffic. Both add income beyond product margin. (older advice, 2020) [L0852.114]
- Affiliate cross promotion is useful when a brand will not approve you as a dropship supplier. An example is a monitor brand that sells to a standing desk store's customers. Affiliate payouts are lower than dropship margins, so use this route for the accessories and upgrades customers still want. [L0923.018]

#### Procedure: Amazon affiliate store on a WordPress theme

1. Sign up for the Amazon Associates affiliate program and copy your unique tracking ID.
2. Buy and install the Shopper Press WordPress theme.
3. In the WordPress admin dashboard, enter your Amazon Associates information and tracking ID in Shopper Press.
4. Choose the Amazon categories you want to sell, for example guitar and its subcategories.
5. Let Shopper Press import the products, including images, prices and descriptions.
6. Match the theme branding to your dropship store, so customers feel they are still on the same brand.
7. Check that the add to cart button sends customers to the Amazon cart with your tracking ID attached.

[L0923.020]

#### Creators testing products

- Some creators test many products on their own accounts and earn affiliate commissions from the ones that take off. This works only when the test content is cheap to produce and the affiliate program pays for the sales. [L1186.013]

### Recruiting affiliates

- Affiliates do not independently discover or approach ecommerce sites. Successful affiliate programs require active recruitment, where you identify potential partners and pitch the program to them. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0215.001]
- The Dream 100 exercise is the primary method for finding affiliates, because it naturally surfaces partnership opportunities during outreach conversations. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0215.002]
- Warrior Forum and JVZoo are job-board platforms where affiliate offers can be posted. These platforms are most effective for limited-time product launches rather than for establishing ongoing affiliate partnerships in traditional high-ticket dropshipping. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0215.009]
- When searching YouTube for affiliates in your product category, examine the video descriptions and check the links for affiliate URL parameters. YouTube creators embed affiliate links in their video descriptions. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0212.011]

### Influencer partnerships

#### Scaling

- Once one influencer partnership works, scale to several parallel influencer partnerships in different niches or markets. The seller reports working with three influencers at once. [L0789.040]
- Influencer partnerships can include paid services beyond promotion, such as TikTok organic growth, content creation and product sourcing. Offering these skills to influencers as added value gives the brand a reason for influencers to accept a deal over competing brands. [L0789.060]
- For a direct-to-consumer brand grown mainly through influencers, about 90% of sales success was attributed to influencer marketing. Influencers were used mostly at the top of the funnel to bring in large volumes of qualified traffic, not only as social proof at the bottom. [L0790.001]

#### Readiness and budget

- Before paying for influencer traffic, set up and test the full sales funnel: store, email capture, retargeting, branded search ads and SEO. Without it, influencer spending is largely wasted. [L0790.004]
- Influencer marketing does not produce quick results. Expect the first year to be a costly testing phase in which some spending does not pay back, and plan the budget for that. [L0790.007]
- Spending very large sums, such as around $100,000 on one big-name creator, is risky without an exceptional and hard-to-find product. Social proof alone rarely justifies it. [L1379.022]

#### Product fit by platform

- Some products do not scale profitably through paid ads. For those, influencer campaigns may be the main way to keep sales going. [L1379.019]
- Dropshipped products often convert poorly through Instagram influencers once buyers realize they are dropshipped. Products from a real small brand that makes its own goods tend to convert better on Instagram. [L1379.020]
- TikTok influencer audiences appeared to buy more often than Instagram audiences for dropshipping products. The speaker presented this as an observation, not a proven rule. [L1379.021]

#### Creator whitelisting and owned characters

- Paid creator whitelisting means running ads from a real creator's account. It requires a revenue share and negotiation. A self-owned fictional character avoids that cost and dependency, so it suits testing or a new brand with a small budget. Creator whitelisting is worth considering when more money is available. [L1476.002]

### Business partners and partnership stores

#### Whether to take a partner

- A business partnership works only when both partners are committed to a bigger vision and have the time to work on it, so that the combined result exceeds what each could do alone. Two partners who each want to work about 10 hours per week in a lifestyle business, often in different time zones, usually do not benefit from a partnership. [L0413.004]
- A business partner should bring more value than hours and have skills that complement yours. For example, one partner may be strong at supplier approvals, phone calls and closing deals, while the other builds websites that convert. [L0413.005]
- Choose a partner who has both succeeded and failed in other ventures. A partner who has only seen success may give up when sales fall short of expectations. [L0413.006]
- Business partnerships rarely suit a new dropshipping store unless a partner brings a large network or a rare, specific skill set. A 50/50 partner takes half the profit forever and half the business value on a sale, while outsourcing a task is paid once. (Source A, 2025) [L1097.001]
- A business partnership is worth considering mainly when the partner brings a large existing network or a rare, specific skill set, or when the partner is a spouse in a close family arrangement. Otherwise, paying an outside provider for a specific task is usually cheaper than giving a partner a permanent share of profit and of the business's sale value. (Source A, 2025) [L1097.044]

**Procedure: agree before starting a business partnership** (in writing)

1. Each partner's exact role and responsibilities, so no two people do the same work.
2. A shared long-term vision and goals. Agree on these before starting, because a partnership is very hard to exit once the business is successful.

[L0413.007]

#### Partnership stores (separate projects)

- An experienced store owner can grow income by partnering with trusted people from their own circle who are interested in the model. The owner oversees operations while each partner does their assigned part. The partnership store is a separate business from the owner's main store. [L1125.001]
- A done-for-you web design service delivers a fully finished, ready-to-sell store. It can be used to launch partnership stores quickly without building them from scratch. [L1125.003]
- In a partnership store, do not give the partner equity or any share of your main business. You are only a co-owner of the new, separate store. [L1125.004]
- Do not bring a partner into your main income source. A split main business with a partner who is not pulling their weight is a much bigger problem than a separate project. Partnership stores work best as future projects. [L1125.006]
- Spreading income across several partnership stores diversifies risk. After three or four such stores, they may even earn more than the main store, so you are not locked into a single business partnership for your lifestyle income. [L1125.007]

**Procedure: partnership model for additional stores**

1. Make sure your first store is already built, has suppliers and traffic set up, and is generating the income you need.
2. Confirm you understand the model and the process well enough to run it yourself.
3. Identify people you know and trust who are interested in starting a business with you.
4. Choose a new, separate store project for the partnership. Do not use your main business for this.
5. Have the store delivered through a done-for-you web design service, or have your team build it.
6. Give the partner their part of the work while you oversee the operations.
7. Share ownership of the separate store, so both of you earn from it and both receive a payout if it is sold.

[L1125.002]

**Case: partnership stores after a first exit**

- A store owner first built a fully owned store and sold it for a large lump sum. He then invited applicants from his own audience to join him, gathered a group in one location, and helped them build stores together as partnerships. He earned from those stores and later sold them as well. [L1125.008]

**Case: consultant turned partner**

- A dropshipping store was built with consulting help from the start and reached about 250,000 USD in sales in its first three months. The consultant stayed on for the first year, then became a partner to scale the business. In the second year of that partnership, annual revenue reached about 11 million USD before the partnership ended. (older advice, 2021-22) [L1502.006]

### Brand, community and cause deals

- Brand deals with complementary companies, such as breweries or tourism operators, become easy once the audience, email list and followers trust the brand. Such companies often approach the brand. The brand should only accept partnerships that suit its community. [L0487.017]
- Brand partnerships on Karo can complement a high-ticket dropshipping store by filling gaps in the catalogue with products you cannot source from a supplier. This adds sales opportunities without inventory risk or fulfillment work. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0869.009]
- Do not use Karo's brand partnership side as a first step if the store is new, because its benefits require sales history and an existing customer base. Start with the free influencer side to test the account. (older advice, 2021-22) [L0869.014]
- A cause-linked model donates one product (for example one pair of reading glasses) for each item sold, through a partner organization. A small per-sale donation is affordable and gives customers and influencers a real reason to support the brand. [L0790.002]
- Hosting community events, such as digital nomad conferences held in several countries, can grow a business and its network at the same time. The largest event in the lesson had about 400 entrepreneurs attending. The series ran seven events across Thailand, Las Vegas and Mexico before COVID ended it. [L0863.010]

## Content, organic traffic and audience ownership

### Content's role in the marketing mix

- Content marketing does not replace paid advertising. It adds another channel alongside paid ads. [L0500.001]
- The marketing system of an e-commerce store is a set of interrelated components that improve each other when combined. Content sits at the centre of this system and makes several other components work better. [L0500.004]
- Content creates options, such as more ways to attract customers, grow the store and keep customers, similar to how money creates options in life. The value comes from the options it opens, not from the content as an end in itself. [L0500.003]
- Content marketing builds trust before the first purchase. Shoppers comparing your store with large marketplaces need a reason to buy from you, and detailed product pages, product videos and educational articles give you a selling point those marketplaces rarely match. [L0380.001]
- An article that ranks for a relevant keyword keeps bringing visitors for as long as the topic has demand, while each paid click stops when the budget stops. Content is therefore a one-time production cost with long-term traffic, as opposed to paying per visit. [L0380.002]
- Organic traffic costs time instead of ad money and can scale slowly, while paid traffic can start immediately and scales with the size of the market. Both should work together, and a store should not rely on only one of them. [L1114.004]

### Outsourcing content production

- Business owners should not create content themselves beyond a minimal amount, because their time is better spent on promoting content. [L0379.001]
- Content creation should be outsourced to freelancers, especially for small to medium e-commerce businesses, where doing it in-house leads to frustration and no progress. [L0379.001]
- A standard content plan is two pieces per month. [L1421.013] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- When an ultimate guide is planned as a truly epic piece, it can replace both monthly pieces as the only content for that month, because its promotion lasts longer. [L1421.013] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- A dropship store can grow from zero to over $20,000 monthly revenue using content marketing alone, without paid advertising, by building high-quality content through outsourced creation and systematised templates. [L0326.001]
- Starting budget reference for content marketing:

| Item | Cost |
|---|---|
| Ahrefs | 14-day free trial, enough for the keyword process |
| Writers (Upwork) | $50 to $100 per 1,000-word article |
| Native English editors | $20 to $25 per hour |
| Sales copywriter for headlines and meta descriptions | $40 to $100 per hour |
| Starting test: 10 articles | $1,000 to $1,500 total |
| Optimising headlines and meta descriptions | about $2,000 |

[L0382.047]

### When to start: sequence with paid traffic

- You can either continue building your audience before monetising, or start selling immediately while continuing to build your audience. Both approaches are valid depending on your specific situation. [L0237.010]
- If paid traffic such as pay-per-click is still profitable, scale it fully before investing in content marketing. Start content as an additional traffic channel if you are looking for alternatives. [L0382.045]
- When paid channels such as PPC, Amazon and Facebook ads are unavailable in a niche, content marketing can become the main source of traffic. This reduces dependence on platforms that restrict the category. [L0382.052]
- Build an audience before the product launch by posting educational content, such as recipes or health advice, on social platforms. This also reveals what the target audience wants. [L1016.014]
- Community building comes after the first sales. Focus first on the first 10 and then 20 sales, and build community with the customers who arrive afterwards. [L1016.015]
- Do not write blog posts to get a supplier approved, because they do not help approvals and only delay revenue. Blog content belongs in the SEO phase, once organic traffic is being built. [L0853.014] *(Older advice, 2021.)*

**Which framing applies:**

| Situation | Approach | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Paid traffic still profitable | Scale paid fully first; add content as an extra channel | L0382.045 |
| Paid channels unavailable in the niche | Content can become the main traffic source | L0382.052 |
| Content as the only channel | A store can reach over $20,000 monthly revenue with content alone | L0326.001 |
| Default guidance | Treat content as an addition to paid channels, plan it alongside other sources from the start, and combine the sources | L0380.036, L0500.001, L1114.004 |

The sources disagree on whether content can carry a store alone. L0326.001 describes a store that reached $20,000 monthly with content only. L0380.036 advises treating content as an addition rather than a guaranteed replacement. The main-channel framing applies where paid channels are unavailable, and the addition framing applies everywhere else, including where paid traffic is still profitable. [L0326.001, L0380.036, L0382.045, L0382.052, L0500.001, L1114.004]

### Case: building the audience before the first offer

- A homebrewing test brand had a parked Shopify site with no products yet while the owner built the audience first. A Facebook page was created, and a meme-style image was posted with a caption asking people to like and share, followed by a Facebook ad with only an engagement objective at about 5 dollars per day. [L0487.014]
- The post reached about 46,000 people, received about 3,100 likes, 71 comments and 349 shares, and the cost per engagement was below one cent. [L0487.014]
- After a few weeks, about 3,600 people had engaged on Facebook and about 5,300 on Instagram, which formed the seed audiences. [L0487.014]
- No offers were planned for roughly six weeks while content was produced. The later plan was to use dropshipping for expensive products, private label for mid-priced products, and a seasonal subscription box. [L0487.014]

### SEO and blog use

- Do not sell ad space on your blog to other companies. Use the traffic and articles to sell your own products instead. [L0382.044]
- Check domain authority (Moz) by comparing the domain authority of sites you could get links from against each other, using the top sites in your niche, instead of a fixed minimum score. [L0856.119]
- Paying for Google Ads to promote blog posts only makes sense at several million dollars in annual revenue. Before that level, it is wasted money. [L1281.028] *(Older advice, 2022.)*
- Building organic traffic through SEO is an important part of high-ticket dropshipping and should not be postponed indefinitely in favour of paid traffic alone. [L0443.006] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- A high-ticket dropshipping business that depends heavily on paid traffic is exposed as soon as ad costs rise or performance drops. Building organic traffic through SEO is a common weakness that is easy to neglect when paid advertising feels more comfortable. [L0443.005] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*

### Owning the audience and the email list

- There are only three types of traffic: traffic you own, traffic you control, and traffic you don't control. The goal for all three is to convert it into owned traffic, such as an email list or a follower base. Growing owned traffic is the core task of the business. [L0523.020]
- Owned traffic such as an email list, followers or past customers can be contacted at no extra acquisition cost. Repeat sales to this audience are essentially pure profit. [L0523.021]
- Owning an audience you can contact at any time, traditionally through an email list, is the long-term goal of marketing. Paid traffic, ads and SEO reach people you do not own, so they do not give the same security. [L0412.001] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Traffic you don't control includes social media, search engine optimisation, guest blogging, YouTube and guest interviews. Send all of it to your own blog, where the top section captures email addresses. Once visitors give an email address, they become owned traffic. [L0523.025]
- A dropshipping store usually runs direct response marketing that pushes for an immediate sale, unlike brands that build awareness first. Cold visitors rarely buy on the first visit, so the store needs a way to keep interested visitors who have not bought yet. Email and SMS capture that group and move them toward a purchase. [L0605.001]

### Risks and dependence on a single channel

- Content marketing carries its own risks and takes sustained effort, so treat it as an addition to paid channels rather than a guaranteed replacement for them. Results arrive slowly, and a single article rarely changes sales. Plan it alongside other traffic sources from the start. [L0380.036]
- Do not depend on one traffic source. Google Ads accounts can be suspended with little warning, especially in industries platforms consider risky, and a store that gets all its visitors from one paid channel can lose nearly all of them within a day. Combine paid channels such as PPC and Shopping with content marketing and email. [L0380.003]
- A business that relies only on paid ads depends on a single traffic source. If the paid channel fails, for example through account problems or rising costs, traffic can stop entirely. Content gives the business other ways to attract visitors. [L0500.002] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Relying only on pay-per-click ads and influencer marketing is a long-term risk. A high-ticket business without content tends to have higher cost per acquisition and limited traffic options. [L0414.022] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Be skeptical of anyone who says content marketing is unnecessary, especially when they sell paid-ad training, because such claims often hide a commercial motive. [L0500.005] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- If someone says they tried content marketing and it failed, the content was most likely not done properly. This is not evidence that content marketing itself does not work. [L0500.006] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*

### Facebook groups and community channels

- Facebook groups cannot be made read-only, so set new posts to require approval to stop questions. Move ongoing support to community channels organised by topic, which stay current. [L0934.114]

### YouTube as an asset

- A YouTube channel with many subscribers and steady engagement adds value to an online business, and that value matters if the owner later sells it. Each extra traffic source, including YouTube, makes the business less dependent on a single channel. [L1511.026] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Case: a drop-shipping store that owns, produces and holds none of its products sold grow lights from many brands, priced from about 150 dollars up to about 1,000 dollars. [L1424.039] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Over roughly two years it built a YouTube channel with reviews, buyer's guides and top-7 lists of its range, and it also embedded these videos on its website. [L1424.039] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- Results: the channel reached about 22,000 subscribers, single videos drew about 21,000 to 35,000 views, the videos probably reached millions of views in total, and the website receives tens of thousands of visits per month. [L1424.039] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*
- The video descriptions link to the products, and the closing section promotes the affiliate program. [L1424.039] *(Older advice, 2021-22.)*

### Faceless AI channels

- A faceless AI-presented channel works well in a niche where a specific language audience has little content of the required type, because the unmet demand is the opening. The creator does not need to appear on camera, which lowers the barrier for people who are not comfortable with their own face. [L0888.002]
- Case: a new faceless YouTube channel was built entirely with AI tools (portrait, script, voice, avatar animation, subtitles), with no on-camera presence by the operator. [L0888.001]
- After two days, the two uploaded videos had about 1,200 views, about 200 likes and 18 new followers across TikTok, and the operator used it to practise organic TikTok growth with a target of 1 million views. [L0888.001]
- AI-presented videos can be turned into digital products, such as full video courses on a topic, product tutorials with a speaking presenter, or audiobooks made from text. An audiobook version can be offered as a free bonus with an ebook to add value for customers. [L0888.003]

### Short-form social presence

- Case: a new account started posting on 5 November with a single new product. The first uploads got about 500 views, then 3,000, 1,000, 1,000, 3,000, 9,000 and 26,000 views. [L1383.038]
- After five days of uploads the account had about 800 followers. One video using the hook *Why did you give me this free product?* reached about 375,000 views within three days. [L1383.038]
- That one video raised followers to about 6,000, viewers began commenting that they wanted the product, and at least one sale was reported. A later upload using the hook *Do you see yourself needing this?* reached about 23,000 views within three hours. [L1383.038]
- Product accounts are easier to grow than personal accounts because their content stays on topic and focused. Build separate product accounts rather than relying on a personal profile. [L1384.034]
- One creator reached 100,000 followers in about one month on a personal account by uploading every day. Personal accounts were considered harder to grow than product accounts, which grew more easily. The product account is the default recommendation, and a personal account can still grow fast with daily uploads. [L1384.033, L1384.034]
- Consistent daily posting is a key growth driver, because most competitors post inconsistently. Consistent upload frequency with slightly better content can outperform many established accounts. [L1384.035]

## Seasonality and Q4 planning

### Planning the Year Around Q4

- Q4 has been the largest quarter for US retail e-commerce sales in almost every year, the exception being 2020, when lockdowns pulled extra demand into another quarter. The US Census quarterly figures used here are unadjusted, and the data runs from 2016 to Q2 2025 in one source and from 2016 to 2025 in another. Plan promotions and inventory around Q4 as the highest-volume season of the year. [L1223.001, L1224.001, L1285.084, L1287.001]
- Sales rise in Q4 even without action, because total buying increases, shoppers buy gifts for themselves and others, and they compare prices online and find smaller stores through competitors' promotions. A live store with no promotions gets only a small Q4 lift. Planned promotions turn that lift into a large spike, and the spike requires coordinated promotions across the website, search ads, social ads and email at the same time. The promotion effort covers five channels: content and SEO, the website, Google Ads, Meta Ads and email. [L1222.001, L1223.001, L1224.002, L1287.002]
- A well-prepared Q4 can bring in roughly the revenue of two other quarters combined, which justifies the extra work of planned promotions. [L1224.003]
- For one brand, about 89 percent of sales from October to mid-December were Christmas gifts, so ad spend should be raised in this period. [L1431.004]
- Q4 order volume for winning products began picking up from September, and the surge arrived in the weeks before the peak season. [L0780.010]
- Q1 is the weakest period for e-commerce, while Q2 to Q4 is the growth season. Test products in Q1 to find winners that can be scaled through Q4. [L1171.037]

### Peak Revenue Months and Peak Count

- Count revenue peaks across the year, such as Black Friday, Mother's Day, Father's Day, Memorial Day weekend or back to school. Aim for at least three peaks per year. If only one or two appear, add promotions or product drops to reach three. [L1235.020]
- Older advice (2018): For established dropshipping stores, November with Black Friday and Cyber Monday is usually the highest-revenue month of the year, while October typically runs at normal levels. [L0466.001]
- Older advice (2018): In December, the first two weeks usually bring most of the month's sales. The week before Christmas is the only real slump, because late orders cannot arrive in time as gifts. Sales soften again around New Year. [L0466.002]
- Older advice (2018): Promotions often start between December 28 and December 31 and lead into a strong January, so plan inventory and ad budgets for that window. [L0466.003]
- Older advice (2018): A store that is set up correctly should not stop selling in December. Treat the holiday period as a normal month, not a reason to pause. [L0466.004]

### Q4 Promotion Calendar

Event dates change by year. Use the table whose weekdays match your calendar year. The 2024 plan below uses Thursday Halloween, Friday Black Friday and Wednesday Christmas.

**Q4 holiday promotion calendar, 2024 plan**

| Promotion | Date | Emails | Remarketing ads |
|---|---|---|---|
| Halloween | Thursday, October 31 | 3 emails on 10/29, 10/30, 10/31, plus 1 extended-offer email on 11/1 | Yes, running alongside the 3 email dates (10/29, 10/30, 10/31) |
| Thanksgiving | Thursday, November 28 | Included with Black Friday | Included with Black Friday |
| Black Friday | Friday, November 29 | 5 emails on 11/25, 11/26, 11/27, 11/28, 11/29 | Yes, running alongside the 5 email dates |
| Cyber Monday | Monday, December 2 | 2 emails on 12/2 AM and 12/2 PM | Yes, running alongside the 2 email sends |
| Christmas | Wednesday, December 25 | 3 emails on 12/9, 12/11, 12/13 | Yes, running alongside the 3 email dates |
| New Year | Wednesday, January 1 | 3 emails on 12/30, 1/1, 1/3 | Yes, running alongside the 3 email dates |

[L1221.001]

**Q4 promotion calendar, 2025 plan**

| Promotion | Date | Emails | Remarketing ad dates |
|---|---|---|---|
| Halloween | Friday, October 31 | 3 | 10/29, 10/30, 10/31 |
| Thanksgiving | Thursday, November 27 (included with Black Friday) | included in Black Friday | included in Black Friday |
| Black Friday | Friday, November 28 | 5 | 11/24, 11/25, 11/26, 11/27, 11/28 |
| Cyber Monday | Monday, December 1 | 2 | 12/1 AM, 12/1 PM |
| Christmas | Thursday, December 25 | 3 | 12/8, 12/10, 12/12 |
| New Years | Thursday, January 1 | 3 | 12/29, 1/1, 1/2 |

[L1222.002]

- End promotions on a Friday when possible, because many customers are paid on Fridays. If a holiday falls on a Thursday, extend the offer through Friday with one more email. [L1285.086]

### Launch Readiness and Lead Times

- Seven weeks is sufficient to build and launch a complete store ready for Q4 traffic if working efficiently. Older advice (2021). [L0270.010]
- Build the store around a sustainable business that can grow over the next decade, rather than chasing a short trend such as a holiday season. A late-starting holiday store can still be launched in a few weeks, but it is a last-minute effort and should not be the main reason to build it. Older advice (2022). [L0429.012]
- Start planning Q4 promotions many weeks ahead, not in the final week before a holiday. Planning five promotions across about three months takes time, and a store launched a few weeks before Halloween still has time to build a list and run them. Planned promotions capture more of the holiday spending, so plan them several weeks ahead rather than days before the holiday. [L1223.002, L1285.085]
- Setting up the store before a Q4 promotion still leaves time to build an email list through abandoned carts and exit overlays before the first promotion goes out. [L1285.099]
- Black Friday and Cyber Monday planning should begin in September, so campaigns, promotions and messaging are ready before the October and November peaks. [L0278.022]
- Winter products need to be ready for the next peak season in advance. Plan a store relaunch in October or early November for Black Friday. Preparing new creatives and an improved store before the season matters more than reacting during it. [L1439.003]
- Plan seasonal products for the whole late-year period, not only for Black Friday, because buyer demand can arrive later in December. One seller made almost nothing on Black Friday but earned most of the season's results from the Christmas period. [L1444.064]

#### Supplier Approvals in December

- Older advice (2018): If you already have approved suppliers, start Google Ads in December rather than waiting until after Christmas. [L0466.031]
- Older advice (2018): If you are still waiting on supplier approvals in December, expect slow responses because staff are on holiday. Use the time to improve the website, build the supplier list, set up social pages and optimize conversions. [L0466.032]

### Black Friday, Cyber Monday and Late-Year Timing
